ParlaMint-AT_1997-06-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00078_d7e3143	What measures are planned by your body in connection with the export offensive? The question submitted in writing has the following wording: [[Quoted print version removed]]
ParlaMint-AT_1997-06-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00078_d7e3175	Please, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-06-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00078_d7e3190	Mr President. High house! I have already presented a programme of around 20 points, which says how I would move forward in terms of export promotion, for weeks before the government clause has dealt with the issue of export offensive. I may make a preliminary remark: It's very important: The basic prerequisite for continuing the export offensive is that we maintain the favourable conditions for Austrian entrepreneurs. It is also to be said in this House that Austria's entrepreneurs are currently operating at the lowest inflation rate since December – 1.2 percent according to EU statistics – with the lowest levels of business taxation, with the lowest interest rates, and for the first time for 80 years in the case of free market entry in all neighbouring countries. There are two institutional barriers to our competitiveness: On the one hand, this is an exaggeration of bureaucracy in procedures that we want to clean or substantially improve under another item on the agenda today, and thus also to reduce them. On the other hand, there is a lack of flexibility rules; I could give the House dozens of examples in this area. We hope that, above all at the social partners, we will be able to make rapid arrangements. In addition, I think that the following points in my area are necessary: I have ordered that experts from my home and the Chamber of Commerce immediately start preparatory work on an information exchange for exporters. It is about the fact that hundreds of exporters who travel daily return with many individual suggestions, which they then forget or drop in the absence of a level capable of mediating. This is about information about customer contacts, new business opportunities, new technologies, deliveries, partner search, representatives. We tested this with a few companies; the response is incredibly positive. I would like to add this to the DAVID system of the Chamber of Commerce. Second point: The fact that Austria's number of exporters has almost tripled since its accession to the EU is increasing in size and demand more effective local assistance by providing rooms and office services, such as desks, telephones or faxes. We are the first project to plan the construction of an Austrian house in Beijing. These surveys have now been used by the companies. A test case will be offered to the branches there in connection with the foreign trade office and, at least, to the Embassy. This branch will be financed from Austrian resources. In addition, discussions with the Austrian Chamber of Commerce and the Austrian banks operating abroad are under way to provide such facilities. Third point: We have found that one of the main difficulties associated with the activity of many small companies abroad is the lack of efficient Austrian bank representations. I say an example: I was on my way recently in some countries, with representatives of small and medium-sized enterprises accompanying me. These companies have been promised a multiple of their current sales of orders and now have incredible financing problems because there is no Austrian bank that can finance this. This point has also been initiated in discussions with the relevant banks. I would remind you that recently, in discussions between the Austrian Economic Delegation, the Bank Austria and the Banco Santander in Madrid, we were assured that in future we could use the extensive Banco Santander network for our Latin American efforts. Next point: Marketing offensive. I have invited relevant economic operators to make proposals for the use of the two amounts that we receive from the BA privatisation, because an analysis of the balance of payments shows that we alone have a trade deficit of 100 billion schilling to the EU, the rest of the world to an asset. This means that we must strive to launch a single market offensive, which, in turn, means that we have to help a few sectors, such as food processing, for example, that the list - I call an example from the recent visit to France - can be achieved at a large French shop in order to achieve market presence. Next point: One of the standard complaints in all hearings with Austrians working abroad is that too few young people are willing to go abroad and take over positions and representations there. We must therefore make a change of course in our training system. Let me give you an example: The technical colleges are to be increasingly taught export science, and appropriate courses are also to be offered in commercial academies. I was recently at an event with almost 200 future graduates of the technical universities in Burgenland. As a second language, all these young people have learned an East language and have already completed internships in Eastern Europe. They come to the labour market at a time when they are still fully flexible, while there are difficulties with others as a result of various obligations after their studies – marriage, house building et cetera. Next point: We've been talking about Consulting Trust Funds for decades. We should ensure that we finally create them. I have a model of participation – consisting of one third of interested economic operators, one third of consuls and one third of the public sector. Another point: In terms of development cooperation, we should make a greater effort to bring about projects in which we can achieve a positive effect through development assistance and economic activity. We have already come a long way in the discussions on the creation of an Asia-Latin America Fund as a venture capital fund for investment in these countries. Above all, there are discussions with the central bank to create better framework conditions for the CIS countries in benchmarking, where Austria is mainly worse than the Federal Republic of Germany. My last two comments – Mr President, I am sorry that it took so long – relate to the foreign trade database in the Ministry of Economic Affairs on travel, interventions and follow-up, and finally – agreed with the Minister of the Interior – to improved treatment for visas and residence permits.

That would have been one of the objectives of this trade order, and I believe that you have missed this objective in a wide range of areas. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] I would like to start, in a reverse way, with my previous speakers, with the area of greenery which is particularly important for us, namely the classical industrial plant law in the industrial system. They have therefore simply reversed the principle that has so far been applied, and only more will those plants which will be designated in the future with a certain regulation undergo a proper procedure. But a proper procedure will be the great exception, there will hardly be any more. In practice, the simplified operating system does not bring the expected administrative simplification and administrative acceleration. ParlaMint-AT_1997-06-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00078_d7e7221	Mr President. Mr. Minister! I believe that the trade regulation is always an attempt to create a relatively fair set of rules in which the interests of consumers are based on a certain minimum quality, the interests of the workers – which are to be regulated, whatever collective agreements are applied et cetera – and the interests of the neighbouring people, as far as neighbourhood rights are concerned, and the interests of the traders themselves are in a reasonable relationship with each other. - That's the one position. The other position is: The trade regulation should in no way be a restriction on access to trade, nor a restriction of competition, not by conscious restrictions on access, but also by unnecessary bureaucracy. In this tension ratio of fair voting, on the one hand, and no restriction on access and no unnecessary bureaucracy, on the other hand, the trade regulations must be drawn up. I believe that a reasonable compromise has been found in this field of tension, all in all. [[Abg. Mr Heindl, Member of the Commission. — (DE) Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I should like to thank the rapporteur, Mr President-in-Office of the Council, for his excellent report. I consider it important that competition should also be introduced in the field of auditors, in which there are always minimum fees. However, it is also necessary to regulate the accounting industry in such a way that not only less qualified activities in the accounting area can be carried out, but that one can at the end of the day reach the balance sheet. Everything else would make no sense and would only be a possibility for certain companies to take outsourcing measures. That would therefore make no sense if the accounting were not also in the process. The workers' questions are quite satisfactoryly solved. The issue of the application of the right collective agreements seems to be satisfactorily solved. In practice, if problems arise, a new innovation would have to be considered. The question of the branch manager is also solved accordingly. The principle must be that no one should be held liable as a business manager who is practically unable to do so. Therefore, this solution is reasonable with the head of the branch, because only those people who, because of their position and their responsibility in the company, can take on practical responsibility. Minister! It is very important to me that it is clear that there must be a reasonable demarcation of self-employed and self-employed activities. This is not, as Mr Haigermoser said at a committee meeting, a struggle of the Chambers for Members, but we want it not to be over the back door social reduction that there is no social reduction by outsourcing, nor by the fact that people who are not in fact self-employed are forced into involuntary self-employedness, so that their employers do not have to comply with holiday and dismissal provisions, but with other social benefits provisions, as well as with the rules on pay, the collective agreement. I see the greatest source of danger in this sector. Minister! They will be adopted, together with the Minister for Social Affairs, a regulation to regulate part-time trade, and I believe that at the beginning we must be very careful. In fact, part-time business should only be allowed if typically delivered or delivered to the end-user. Examples are: the bicycle mechanic, the alternatory, the maintenance of shoes et cetera. However, if there is primarily the possibility to extract from the chain of a production process a sub-sector and then not to supply or provide to the final consumer, but to continue producing it within a business area, then this sub-sector should not be allowed in this first phase. For example, I call the installation of windows and doors, which happens primarily, of course, at new construction sites within a production process. There should not be the possibility of sub-businessing. I say it again: It should only be possible to make the sub-sector possible if it is actually delivered or delivered to the final consumer. We do not want a society – some dream of it – in which there is a third self-employed, but again half of which are involuntarily self-employed, a third workers and a third project worker – I have heard this at a lecture – with the project workers in reality being the day-to-day workers of the third millennium. We do not want to stumble into such a society. Finally, I can only quote the Nobel Laureate Lester Thurow, who described the American labour market. In his description, he says that there are already 8 million people in the U.S. economy who are now working as self-employed entrepreneurs or consultants, but most of these 8 million would be happy to find a right job with normal wages and normal supplementary benefits, such as pension entitlement, health insurance et cetera. So: Self-employment, yes, but only if it is really self-employment, but no to push out self-employed workers into a Quasi self-employment to operate social reduction across the back door. It is not intended to serve this purpose. If it is true that the sub-sector is a possibility for social reduction, then we must also make corrections in this area. Last point: This trade regulation will certainly remove obstacles. It will help to increase the competitiveness of the Austrian economy and thus create additional jobs. In the end, however, we should always be aware that the level of employment, whether jobs are secured or not, depends on whether consumers can also provide the products and services offered. And that then leads to very different questions than those of the trade order. I'm just saying this because we should leave the church in the village: The trade order is good and right, but it will not be a miracle means of creating additional employment. Thank you.

At that time, 1859, this was possibly still regulated in a quite contemporary way. I would like to inform you at least some of the official redundancies in force at the time, Mr Minister, of whom I believe they should not at all be regulated in a commercial regulation. To this end, we have a working labour law, which also regulates redundancies. I don't need a business order to do that. But as far as I can see from the relevant explanations in the book of Friedrich Kuderna on the right of dismissal, there are also facts of this old right of dismissal which are still used for the reasons in a modern Judicature, and I think this is unacceptable. For example, this then § 82 of the Commercial Regulations provides that a worker is threatened with dismissal if he betrays a business or business secret – we have regulated it elsewhere – or without the consent of the trader, one of the uses of the business-responsible side-by-side business is operated. Minister! Now you know as well as I do: What kind of activity is it about? It's about workers. For example, it's about a flower binder or what I know, which works for about four hours and possibly works in another flower binder or gardening for three hours. To this end, this old commercial law of 1859 says: This is unacceptable if the employer does not agree. I would like to point out that, when it comes to such work, the competition clause which has already been applied in such work is also entirely in addition to that. But it's about other things. For example, in the case of a person suffering from a deterrent disease, for example, from external dejection or from another deplorable disease, there is a state of dismissal in accordance with the commercial order of 1859. With confidence, I consider that a modern labour law as superfluous as anything! There are other acts in the trade order which are threatened with dismissal: gross insult to honor, injury to the body, dangerous threat, alcohol consumption et cetera. Or if someone is careless about fire and light, regardless of the prior warning, this is a state of dismissal! It's clear to me now. In 1859, there may have been such activities where, in the event of open fire or light, there may have been any unforeseen reactions in the holding and the operation or other workers have been threatened. Minister! But we are in 1997, and the conditions in the establishments have changed thanks to God. It is also possible, under modern labour law, to release persons who behave grossly negligently. You know that. But this does not need to be regulated in a commercial order, in an undisclosed annex of a commercial order of 1859. The absurdity is that all the changes in the trade regulations of this part have always been taken over or forgotten. I would like to inform you, Mr Minister, and, of course, the parties here in Parliament, of the following motion for a resolution: [[Quoted print version removed]] This is quite simply an attempt to make this old 1859 Kram, which has nothing to do with modern commercial law anyway, because commercial law does not have the task of regulating labour relations, which contains provisions for 1997, for the beginning of the 21st century. ==References====External links==* Official website I believe that it could be a common concern of the parties represented here in Parliament to eliminate an annex to a commercial order of 1859, which is still valid, as workers are insured, such as Mr Kuderna, President of the Supreme Court. This point does not stand in the way of modern commercial law, but it is simply an unworthy thing to try to maintain these points and remain valid. This, of course, also creates situations for workers which may hinder, restrict or threaten them with dismissal in the exercise of their activities. What should and must apply to commercial enterprises, this must also apply to workers employed in commercial enterprises – only those who are meant here. That is why I would ask the parties here in the House to take this motion for a resolution, which does not mean a revolution in the state of dismissal, but simply demands that the status of dismissal laid down in the Constitution be taken into account and approved by the workers alone.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-06-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00078_d7e13285	Mr President. Mr. Federal Chancellor! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Today, three years ago, more than 66 percent of Austrians voted in favour of Austria's accession to the European Union. And just today, the liberals are making a urgent question to the Chancellor of the Federal Republic of Germany in the matter of European monetary union. Ladies and gentlemen! Unfortunately – as is usual with the "F" – this request is only partially supported by factual considerations, speckled with incorrect and half-right facts and is obviously intended to strengthen the uncertainty of the Austrian population, which is unfortunately still present through too little information and material presentation. With your uncertainty campaign, you are doing a bad service to our people! [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] The history of the last 25 years of the European Union or of the European Communities is characterised by the constant efforts of the Member States towards closer cooperation among themselves and the strengthening of political relations to protect their central achievements in the common market. The provisions on economic and monetary union in the Maastricht Treaty reflect many of the findings that have been gained over the past 25 years. The turbulence of the European currencies in the early 1970s made the desire of the Member States to cooperate in monetary policy more and more. Finally, this led to the establishment of the European Monetary System. As a result, the exchange rate fluctuations were reduced by about half. After the introduction of the internal market, i.e. after the signing of the Maastricht Treaty, it became clear to all the States concerned that the introduction of a single European currency is essential as a complement and for the full functioning of the internal market. This Maastricht Treaty aims to make the single currency a stable, yes, I want to say, the most stable currency in the world. Strong and balanced economic and monetary decision-making bodies set the goal of a single currency comparable to the currencies of the Member States with the best economic foundations and set a precise and realistic timetable. Well, ladies and gentlemen, why is there a single currency in Europe? Firstly, to ensure stability and prosperity. Secondly, as an essential complement to the already existing internal market. Thirdly, as an incentive for growth and employment. The public deficits must be kept under appropriate control and the independent European Central Bank will ensure price stability. Fourth: Cost savings. Foreign exchange transactions, security of exchange rate risks are lost, and this means that approximately 0.4 to 0.5% of the Union's gross domestic product will no longer be applied in the future. For Austria, this is about 12 to 13 billion schilling annually. The cost of accession is estimated at 8 billion, which means that each year much more is saved than the one-off costs. A comparison on the basis of which one sees why it is absolutely necessary to introduce the euro: If you convert 1 000 S into the 15 states of the European Union, from Schilling to D-Mark, from D-Mark to Belgian Franc and so on, you only have 55 percent after the 15 conversions, i.e. 550 S. This alone shows that a single European currency is absolutely necessary. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] It is also an essential contribution to international stability. The European Union is the world's largest trading power and we are fighting about 49 percent of the world's total trade. The euro will therefore become one of the most important trading and reserve currencies in the future. Let's just look at our recent past: In part, the United States of America exported its economic difficulties over the dollar's exchange rate. We all know that the exchange rate of the dollar is about 12 s today, if you are in America, he has the purchasing power of about 16 p. That the American goods exported to us are of course favourable, is obvious. Sixthly, something that seems to me to be very important: a strengthened common currency sovereignty. In the European Central Bank – where all 15 Member States are represented – an Austrian representative will be present as Governor – Austria can decide and decide. Let us consider our current situation: We are currently in de facto in a monetary union with the Deutsche Mark, and the Oesterreichische Nationalbank can at best understand decisions of the Deutsche Bundesbank, but not co-decision. [[Intercession with the liberals.]] We are also represented in the most important institutions of economic and monetary union, such as the Council of Economic and Financial Ministers, which jointly define the broad guidelines of economic policy, and also in the European Central Bank system. The central banks are members of the European Central Bank and are independent. It shall not in any form receive instructions from Member States or from Community institutions. This also means price stability on the inside and monetary stability on the outside for the future. Of course, the preliminary work for accession to the monetary union has not yet been completed in Austria. In the context of equal opportunities in the common economic and monetary union, for example, the situation of Austrian credit institutions was highlighted. We have disadvantages over European competition, for example in the minimum reserves of the primary funds of the Austrian credit institutions to be set aside at the National Bank. Depending on time, Austrian credit institutions have to pay 5 percent or – over 36 months – 3 percent. A comparison with the Federal Republic of Germany: There the German credit institutions pay up to 36 months 2 percent, i.e. one third, and beyond that 1.5 percent, half. It is in this area that equal opportunities must be established. One important question: What would Austria be without the euro? – The enormous economic importance of the EU area for Austria is clear from external trade statistics. Two thirds of all Austrian exports of goods go towards the European Union. Almost 70 percent of imports come from this region to Austria. That is why we have a vital interest in being involved in the first step of European monetary union, a much greater than countries with a lower EU linkage. [[Abg. Aumayr: EU expert!]] [[Further calls to the liberals.]] – Listen, ladies and gentlemen, you hear something from practice here! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] The main concern of the Austrians is: Does the money with the euro remain worth the same? – This question must be answered with a yes, because monetary union is in the truest sense not a monetary reform, but a changeover. When the changeover is made, only the name is changed and the amount is exchanged at a previously fixed price. Instead of paying for a thing 100 S, it is then at an accepted price of 13 S per Euro 7.69 Euro for which you receive the same goods. Instead of having 100 000 S on a savings book, you have around 7,692 euros. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Dr. Haider.]] The same logic applies to loans: 10 000 euros instead of 130 000 S, Mr Haider. There is also no difference whether an Austrian receives for his Schilling or a German for his D-Mark Euro. Very briefly on the internal and external stability criteria. Internal stability is measured at the inflation rate. The schilling has never been as stable as today. Let's remember: In 1974 we had an inflation rate of 10 percent in Austria. In 1996, the value was 1.9 percent, and this year we expect 1.7 percent.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-06-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00078_d7e14564	Mrs Ing. Langthaler. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-06-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00078_d7e14585	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! I was very surprised when I heard in the morning that there was a urgent question on the subject of European monetary union. I think it is right that we are discussing the issue of the common European currency in this House this week before the Amsterdam Government Summit, and also about the conditions, such as the question of how much it succeeds in finally integrating the question of employment directly into European Union policy, and also what is meant by it. I therefore very much welcome the opportunity to bring this urgent matter to the House. Also the urgent is extremely well made. What has disappointed me more is what the liberals have given me in concrete terms, because these were actually the old-known examples from the social security institutions to the subject of who are the diligent and decent people in this country and who are not. So it is not really what the Dringers promised in their introduction that they are speaking in the applicant's speeches. Nevertheless, there is no point in discussing the European level and also the common European currency, and I therefore welcome it. For me, the introduction of the urgent ones is one of the central areas of tension in which the debate on a common European currency is moving. On the one hand, the creation of a European monetary union is seen as the great challenge, as the century project has been misregarded, and on the other hand, monetary union is often qualified as the largest monetary experiment in economic history. It is precisely in this field of tension that the arguments, the pro and the counter, and I do not hide that I am on the side of the advocates of a common European currency, although I also believe that it is simply necessary to take far more seriously the legitimate concerns and fears of the people who have been felt and seen in the last few weeks, especially in those countries in which there have been elections. What has been seen – and this has been triggered by the debate on a common currency – is that there has suddenly been a politicization of all of this European policy. The European agreement on economic harmonisation has necessarily come. You may regret that, but it's just a fact. From the first Treaties started in the then EC until today, of course, there is a predominance of harmonisation in the economic field, because it has failed to agree more quickly on a common denominator in policy areas. To date, Europeans have not been able to define what the political Union is supposed to look like. The common foreign policy is more a drama than something in which we see concrete approaches to a truly progressive common European Union policy. Where it has worked and where there has been progress in recent years – however one may interpret it – in the sense of integration, it has undoubtedly always been in the economic field. And the logical consequence of this development is, of course, a common European currency. But not only for economic reasons would they strive. I have also considered the economic sectors more precisely, especially in terms of what happened in Europe if, after these many preparatory steps, there were no common European currency. What would be the consequences for the various markets? It is already seen in an announcement by the French Minister for Finance on the Stability Pact this week, how incredible the impact on European markets, how the D-Mark has suddenly attracted, which Franc and the dollar have lost. Thus, minor expressions lead to incredible eruptions. This would also, of course, be possible if the common European monetary union project were to be abandoned. But what I thought was much more problematic is that in total, the moment it is about in terms of integration, for – as I mean – many years should be abandoned. There are times that are then called "historic" where a really big step is possible. There are a lot of factors together. I think it is still an important factor in Germany that Helmut Kohl is there German chancellors and that it is actually something like a life work for him and that there was also a promise from the Germans to participate in such a common monetary policy in order to actually anchor Germany in the Union in the sense that it does not take a hegemonial position within the European Union. I would like to ask all the critics, not the critics and skeptics of the single currency, because I can understand that, but I would really like to ask the real opponents of a single currency how they not only imagine the alternative, but the role that Germany would have in Europe. It is therefore no wonder to me that the President of the Federal Bank in Germany is actually one of the biggest critics of this common currency and that there are many critics on the part of the CSU. For me, this is more about chauvinism and certainly not a European dimension, which can at least be seen in their arguments there. I thought it was a huge problem for the future of Europe not to involve Germany with its strong financial and economic strength, which is easy within Europe, in fact to bind so firmly with a common currency that no one-sided German foresight in Europe – neither economic policy nor, above all, political – is the more possible. For me, the euro is not just an economic project, but almost more a political project, a political project in which it is really about achieving an integration that is irreversible. In my opinion, a European monetary union would indeed be a major step towards a European identity. This is seen in the United States, where both the dollar and, above all, the language are the greatest identifiers. You can see this in the case of immigrants coming to America. Of course, it is the dollar and the language that make it possible to create historical differences and developments in such a large country with such great structural differences and, in part, large – of course not as big as in Europe – a common identity that is simply extremely important for a society, if it is so large and has so complex to manage. A European currency would actually create a European identity, and that is just one of the positives for me. But I do not want to hide that I see that there are also many potential areas of problem. It is a fact that there will be losers in the first months and years with a common currency. It is a fact that we cannot introduce a European currency beyond the concerns of the population, and that is why we must take these results more than seriously in the UK and France. I am very pleased that there are now left-wing Heads of Government on the row, and I am particularly pleased that this pressure, which is mainly due to France, is at last being seriously discussed in the European Union. On its own, it is seen that even in the case of monetary union critics, at least it can be seen as positive that, because of the need for fiscal consolidation, because of the need to achieve the Maastricht criteria, there is now a pressure in the population, which is also an opportunity. And there is, rightly, a dissatisfaction, because this pressure is due to, in part, enormously rapid structural adjustment in these countries, which in many areas are too fast, too harsh and, above all, too much at the expense of the weak levels of incomes, but it is this pressure that finally succeeds in seriously discussing a European social policy and a European employment policy. We are only at the stage of discussion, Mr Gusenbauer, but even if we get a chapter on employment in the Treaty, I cannot yet be convinced that this has a direct impact. But the awareness seems to have come into the minds of the European governments in particular, and it must succeed in bringing it not only into the consciousness of the minds, but into the concrete government policy, so that it succeeds in creating a truly common employment policy, which in the future also means public investment – more than it has been in the last two or three years – and a common European currency. I am still optimistic that it can succeed in staying on schedule. I would very much like it because I fear that if the euro does not come according to the prescribed timetable, it will not come for a long time. I thought this was very bad for Europe, and I thought it was bad for Austria too.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-06-12-020-XX-NRSITZ-00078_d7e16760	Mr President. Estimated members of the Federal Government! Ladies and gentlemen! Today's decision on the trade regulation also requires adjustments to the vocational training law. In order to create new incentives for apprentice training for the firms, in addition to the examination of trainers, the completion of an apprenticeship course will also be considered as proof of qualifications for apprentice training. Another training attempt, the so-called shortened training period for certain groups of people with higher-qualified education, is also being transferred to the general education system. But it seems particularly important to me to note one thing, ladies and gentlemen: We here in Parliament can only create the legal framework. I believe that the economy and the enterprises, if they continue to act so restrictively in apprenticeship training, will cut themselves off the branch on which they sit. It is precisely dual vocational training – and this has been said by my previous speaker as well as by the Federal Minister today – that is a great advantage for Austria's economic location, as we have well-trained skilled workers and professionals. I am thinking of Upper Austria, for example the example BMW in Steyr. The crucial factor in BMW's having just settled in this region was not only that federal and country-based funding was paid – these subsidies would have been given to BMW elsewhere – but that well-trained professionals are present in this region. I believe that this is a very important, essential factor that we must also consider for the future. I would like to take this opportunity to make some critical comments, because it makes no sense to stress the positive aspects here all the time. The current situation is as follows: In recent years, the willingness of the Austrian apprenticeships to train apprenticeships within the dual system has decreased by a quarter. By the end of last year, only 39,700 establishments were still in the school of apprentices in 1984. This withdrawal of the Austrian firms from apprenticeship training is also reflected in the decline in apprenticeship numbers, as is generally known. If it were still about 170,000 apprentices in 1985, at the end of last year only about 120,000 apprentices were trained. In 1992, Austria ratified the International Convention on the Rights of the Child. One of the essential contents of this Agreement is the implementation of the rights of children and young people on the basis of equal opportunities. Quality, education and training, which corresponds to the talents and interests of young people, is essential for both personal development and the development of democracy and the economy. It must therefore be our common concern to ensure that children and young people at all levels of education and training are provided with a sufficient number of schools and training places. The amendment that we are deciding today can only be a further step towards a modern framework. Apprenticeship training is intended to carry out four fundamental tasks in the interests of individual people, the economy and society, and thus also of our state. First of all, these are the most widespread prevention of youth unemployment – that is, as I believe, undisputed; that is our all concern – second, training in a profession corresponding as far as possible to the suitability and inclination of young people, taking into account economic and labour market requirements in our country, third, a smooth transition from school to working life through the integration of school leavers into working life and the creation of the basis for entry into the labour market after the end of the teaching period, and fourth, a sound basis for continuing vocational qualification. Apprenticeship training therefore requires a fundamental reform with an adaptation to the existing framework conditions and a further development into a modern, future-oriented vocational training system. This could also increase the acceptance of apprenticeship training in the public. The dual vocational training system is considered to be essential even today. It must, however, offer a sensible and objectively sound alternative to the full-time vocational school and adequately expand or complete the opportunities to acquire initial vocational qualifications. Austria – and we are proud of it – has always been able to point to the least youth unemployment in Europe in recent years. The crucial reason for this is the highly qualified training of our young people. The proposed amendment to the Child and Youth Employment Act will continue to ensure and maintain the high level of protection for apprentices. Today's decision is a step towards securing and creating jobs. But one thing is quite clear – and we should ask ourselves to do so: There's still a lot of work in front of us! Thank you.

This also includes the wide range of offers made possible by the new Vocational Training Act. An initiative is being launched to offer training places to local authorities and associations. Advisors will be employed in the establishments specifically engaged in the acquisition of teaching institutions. A model for an apprenticeship fund is being developed, which is intended to be a collection network, with the help of young people who, after the 1. In November, there was no teaching space for dual training. Models are being set up for vocational training so that young people who already have vocational training, or who already have a HAK or HTL, or who, if necessary, already have a degree in higher education, but do not find a job, can make an internship in a profession. It has been shown that young people who already have an internship in addition to their vocational training find a job easier. All these initiatives show that it is a major concern for us that young people do not begin their professional life with the experience that they are superfluous. It is intended to prove to young people that they have a chance of training, of completing an internship and of getting into work. The professional maturity test that we decide today represents a gap between permeability and permeability for me – in order to remain with a modern diction. It is a recognition of the key qualifications acquired in the profession, and it is also a further step towards the recognition of existing skills and knowledge. If all of us together have a positive impact on the possibilities associated with the professional maturity test, then this professional maturity test will certainly contribute to the upgrading of teaching professions in the public, especially with parents!

Setting up your own "special schools for the most gifted" how Karl-Heinz Gruber has characterized the conservative schools of high-privileged persons, which are often called upon by conservatives, does not seem to be a good way to us either, because this promotes social isolation, although learning would be called for social responsibility. Ladies and gentlemen! We can therefore imagine that a child who is able to complete the learning material of a year in a shorter period of time will be given the opportunity to continue his specific skills within the school year, perhaps also combining it with a stay abroad and learning a language. [[Congratulations at the Liberal Forum.]] You see, therefore, that from our point of view, the promotion of talent is not the development of new curriculum or even new schools, but rather a greater flexibility of existing institutions. The ideology that all children in one class have to learn the same in principle should alter the goal of applying a lot of imagination in order to meet the different talents and educational expectations of young people. [[Congratulations at the Liberal Forum.]] Finally, I would like to focus briefly on the request for suspension of the spelling reform. To deal now with the sensuality of this reform, I think it is obsolete, because time has really gone over it. Mr Schweitzer. A ten-year discussion between nationally authorized expert committees from all German-speaking countries and countries with German-speaking minorities was followed by a re-regulation of the spelling, which took place on 1 January. It was decided on 1 July 1996. The joint declaration of intent on the re-regulation of German legislation provides for the rule to be applied at 1. A transitional period until 31 August 1998 is expected to take effect. ==References====External links== The main focus of the reform is on systematic editing and recasting of the rules of German spelling, without affecting the tradition of writing culture and the legibility of the texts. We Liberals are committed to this reform! It first and foremost provides a relief to pupils from the empty learning of a complicated rule. I also admit that, in our view, a much greater simplification of the spelling would have been desirable. Unfortunately, this was not possible due to the need for political compromises. Ladies and gentlemen! Finally, the new discussion on copyrights by the Association of Authors: Currently, for the use of literary works for educational purposes, there is a general right of authors to the state, according to which the spelling or style of literature is given priority over intervention in the administrative interest. If, now, the Austrian Association of Authors represents the view that the general granting of rights does not include the adaptation of its texts to the new rules of spelling, this position is to be given priority from our point of view of the word – in the truest sense of the word. Our position on this is therefore: Texts for which copyright claims exist are to be published in the school books requested by copyright holders in that legal form. These texts can be identified accordingly, so that pupils are able to perceive differences in the law. It is also to be welcomed from a liberal point of view when young people realize that spelling is dynamic and can change continuously. From our point of view, it is also very useful for students to be able to see that the validity of these national rules can reach borders outside the state. - Thank you.
